# R2 vs Tigris for tiles.immich.cloud misses: cold, warm and cached from every location we could reach

Measured 2026-11-08 22:04Z to 2026-11-09 02:43Z (European night, Asia-Pacific Monday morning). All three test hosts run the gateway, cached Tiles entrypoint and 120 ms race of [#489](https://github.com/immich-app/geoshenanigans/pull/489), with prod D1 read-only. They differ only in where they read chunks from. Every number below is copied or formatted from the stage output files listed at the end. Times are UTC, latencies in ms, CIs are 95%.

**Verified 2026-11-09 ~03:15Z** against the raw Globalping JSONL, the shared ledger and the cost scripts (verify stage, `r2poc/verify/`). The e2e, matrix, miss-breakdown and cost numbers reproduce. Corrections and additions are marked *[verify]* and listed in section 12; the unedited report is kept with the raw data.

## 1. The answer

- **Cold misses (never-requested tile):** R2 with a bucket per colo (`reg`) beats Tigris (`g`) on traffic-weighted firstByte: 330 vs 431 ms, reg−g −115 [−137, −90]. One weur bucket (`one`) gets 388 (one−g −39 [−58, −3]). *[verify]* The differences are weighted medians of paired per-probe differences, so they are not the gap between the two weighted medians (431 − 330 = 101).
- **Where:** Europe −152 [−184, −132] (one and reg both read weur there) and North America −73 [−108, −20]. Asia (+2 [−75, +74]), Oceania (−101 [−142, +7]) and South America (−33 [−70, +207]) show no significant difference; Africa is −181 [−188, +23] from 5 probes. The single weur bucket is slower than Tigris outside Europe: NA +82, AS +142, OC +472.
- *[verify]* **East and Southeast Asia, where the Asian traffic is:** the Asian median mixes 21 East/Southeast Asian probes with 13 West/Central Asian ones (Middle East, Caucasus, Central Asia), whose misses run at FRA on weur. In East/Southeast Asia alone (HKG, SIN, NRT, KIX, TPE, ICN, BKK, CGK, MNL and others; about 84% of the measured colos' Asian traffic), Tigris is faster on cold misses: reg−g +44 [+7, +117] (21 probes; levels g 536 / one 738 / reg 614), and on cold2 +163 [+32, +297]. R2 per colo wins there only on warm misses (−95 [−171, −2]). The West/Central Asian probes carry 0.7% of traffic and go the other way (reg−g −149).
- **Warm misses (same tile, CDN miss, storage range just read):** reg 187 / one 232 / g 263, reg−g −52 [−73, −36].
- **Why, and the catch:** most of R2's lead is Tigris opening a new HTTPS connection in idle PoC isolates: g's cold storage read took 272 ms against 149 ms primed at the same colo. On a second cold tile straight after (cold2), reg−g shrinks to −38 [−69, +7], and Tigris wins in Asia (+92 [+13, +265]). On a primed connection the R2 read alone is not faster: the weighted cold read at the tier is 172 (reg; 174 with the deployed map, see 4.2) and 230 (one) against 155 (Tigris). The real gap depends on how often production misses find a warm Tigris connection. This PoC could not measure that.
  - *[verify]* A steady trickle of misses at one tier colo did not warm Tigris connections in the PoC. At FRA, g tier misses that came less than 3 s after another g tier miss at FRA still took 238 ms for the Tigris read (median, n = 62), against 132–140 ms primed (matrix, tier only / both sides). Only a probe's own follow-up request (cold2) got faster, which points to reuse along the same machine path, as inside one user's burst of tiles. So cold2 (reg−g −38 [−69, +7]) is the better guide for misses inside a burst. The warm-connection model in 6.3 (g 293) is the best case for Tigris, not an expected value.
- **Not fast everywhere:** R2's apac bucket takes 263–458 ms for a cold read even from Asian colos. Its cold reads cost 217–286 ms more than warm ones even at its best colos (NRT, KIX), against about 48–61 ms for weur and 77–131 ms for the other buckets (4.4). *[verify, corrected]* Indian misses all ran at BOM, where the six buckets' median cold reads were 615–734 ms (28 calls from Indian probes; 622–766 over all 54 BOM-tier calls), against 302–311 ms for Tigris. End to end, South America is 734–778 ms (continent medians) and the Indian colos 712–1,067 ms (3 samples per colo) on every host. Oceania pays about 290 ms for D1 on every host, because no Oceania D1 replica ever answered, despite the pre-warm.
- **Cached (Workers Cache hit):** the same on all hosts: 34 / 35 / 35 ms, reg−g +1 [−1, +3]. That is expected.
- **European map sessions (two European vantages only):** R2 lowers mean view completion by 8–9 ms against Tigris (p90 −34 ms); reg and one are identical there.
- *[verify]* **Against the Fly PoC (2026-11-08):** the two runs used the same aggregation but different windows (Sunday 13:51–16:53Z there, Monday 00:21–02:10Z here) and probe sets. On the 33 client colos both runs measured, never-requested tiles took reg 319 / one 368 / g 435 here, and ft 167 / ftr 165 / prod 366 / g 506 there. Against each run's own g: reg −116, ft −339. So R2 per colo is roughly 150–220 ms slower than the Fly fleet behind Workers Cache on never-requested tiles (152 raw; 223 with each taken against its run's g). Prod was not measured in this run, and g moved by 71 ms between the windows, so this PoC cannot say whether R2 beats today's prod on misses (section 11).
- **Cost at 35.8M requests/month (cost stage):** #489 on Tigris $40.9, on one R2 bucket $38.2 ($2.7 less), on six R2 buckets (the `reg` layout) $59.6. Today's setup costs $29.4.

## 2. Method and definitions

### Classes

| class | request | Workers Cache | storage |
|---|---|---|---|
| **cold** | a never-requested tile whose chunk nothing in this PoC had read from that storage; the chunk is reserved in the shared ledger before the read | miss at both tiers | cold read |
| **warm** | the same tile again with a unique `&nc=<token>`, same probe, about 9 s after the cold request (p50 9 s, max 18 s) | miss at both tiers (all 1,662 carried X-Miss-Path with the cold tile's key) | the object was read about 9 s earlier, but *[verify]* by the same exec colo only 44–49% of the time, and the tier/local path flipped in 37–40% of units, so it is often another colo, isolate and connection. Every warm miss did its own D1 lookup (0 of 1,662 from memory) |
| **cached** | the exact cold URL again, at least 3 s later, same probe; it had to come back `Cf-Cache-Status: HIT` | hit at the client colo | not used (control) |
| **cold2** (pass 3 only) | a second never-requested tile from the same probe, right after warm | miss at both tiers | cold object, but the path (isolates, storage connection) had just carried this probe's requests |

### Hosts

| host | chunks read from | Worker version | URL |
|---|---|---|---|
| **g** | Tigris tiles-geo `20261101/` (endpoint by the request's continent: t3.storage.dev for EU/NA/AS, fly.storage.tigris.dev elsewhere) | 4afd5502 | `tiles-poc-r2-g` (workers.dev, deleted after the run) |
| **one** | R2 tiles-poc-r2-weur | b3a85a9e | `tiles-poc-r2-one` (workers.dev, deleted after the run) |
| **reg** | R2, one of six buckets per colo from the measured map (308 colos, default weur) | 7300a581 (map deployed 00:07:30Z) | `tiles-poc-r2-reg` (workers.dev, deleted after the run) |
| probe | storage matrix: reads all 8 storages at the tier colo and the client colo | v2 (10e0ed85) | `tiles-poc-r2-probe` (workers.dev, deleted after the run) |

- All hosts: #489 + 120 ms race (variant g at 19cd692), prod D1 `tiles` read-only, workers.dev only.
- All hosts also carry the prepare stage's measurement-only changes (`prep/patches/g-measurement-changes.diff`): the `?nc=` forced miss, the X-Miss-Path chunk key, range and D1 fields, and `?resolve=1`.
- **Deployments** (checked with wrangler): g 4afd5502 and one b3a85a9e since 23:18Z, reg 7300a581 since 00:07:26Z, probe 10e0ed85 since 23:17:45Z. None changed during the measurements.
- **R2 buckets:** tiles-poc-r2-{wnam,enam,weur,eeur,apac,oc}. The API reports each at its hint's location.
- **Copy:** each bucket holds all 138,883 live chunks (138,882,437,231 bytes), copied from Tigris by the Helsinki box through an uploader Worker that ran in WAW, 22:04:14–22:34:33Z.
- **Verify** (22:35–22:54Z): object counts and bytes match; a full key/size/etag (MD5) compare found 0 mismatches in every bucket; 300 full-object MD5s and 120 range reads matched Tigris.
- **Location hints:** the R2 docs still list only wnam, enam, weur, eeur, apac and oc, with nothing for South America, Africa, the Middle East or India. Hints are best effort.
- **Read path:** gateway, then the metadata service, then the tiered read cache, then storage in the bucket's region. The docs do not say whether that storage runs on Oracle or on Cloudflare's own hardware, and these measurements cannot tell.

### D1 pre-warm

- **Warmer:** tiles-poc-r2-d1warm ran from 22:51:45Z and is still running. It has nine Durable Objects placed with location hints. Each sends `SELECT 1` through a D1 session (first-unconstrained) K times per second.
- **K history** (prepare stage log):
  - oc and eeur went to K=20 at 23:06Z and K=50 at 23:21Z, with no change in routing.
  - At 23:36Z oc went back to K=20 and eeur to K=5.
  - The eeur Durable Object runs in ARN, which D1 routes to the WEUR replica in LHR. The EEUR replica in FRA was up anyway and served FRA, CDG, WAW and OTP.
  - *[verify]* The warmer did not demonstrably change any routing. WNAM, ENAM and APAC answered from replicas from its first minute (prepare stage), Oceania never did at K=5, 20 or 50, and the eeur Durable Object always went to WEUR. The replicas that served were up anyway, so the pre-warm cannot be credited with them.

Who answered each warmer's queries, pooled over the e2e stage's 10 snapshots (00:21–02:10Z, 10-minute windows):

| hint | DO colo | K | answered by (10-min windows, summed) |
|---|---|---|---|
| wnam | SEA | 5 | WNAM:0=29940 |
| enam | ATL | 5 | ENAM:0=29992 WEUR:1=3 |
| sam | ORD | 5 | ENAM:0=29995 |
| weur | LHR | 5 | WEUR:0=30000 |
| eeur | ARN | 5 | WEUR:0=29990 WEUR:1=10 |
| apac | HKG | 5 | APAC:0=29995 |
| oc | BNE | 20 | WEUR:1=119980 |
| afr | LHR | 5 | WEUR:0=29995 WEUR:1=5 |
| me | OTP | 5 | EEUR:0=29990 |

Latest snapshot (02:44Z, `prep/logs/d1warm_latest.txt`): unchanged. The oc Durable Object in BNE still sends 100% to the WEUR primary in MAD, p50 365 ms.

**Measured status:**
- WNAM (SJC), ENAM (ORD), WEUR (LHR), EEUR (FRA) and APAC (HKG and NRT) replicas served lookups throughout.
- No Oceania replica served at any time. Section 7 has the per-region detail.

### Probes, pinning and aggregation

- **Globalping HTTP tests:**
  - The storage matrix made one test per Globalping city: 1,114 cities, with a second call for every ok city in AS, ME, OC, SA and AF.
  - The end-to-end stage used 185 pinned probes: one per client colo the matrix found (126), plus 59 on other networks in the 30 busiest colos.
  - A probe is pinned by reusing an earlier measurement id as the location of later tests.
- **Quota:** the first 487 tests ran on the two free per-IP quotas (this machine and the box). After that a Globalping token was used; credits went from 20,000 to 14,469 by the end of the e2e stage. There were no HTTP 429s.
- **Metrics:**
  - End-to-end: Globalping firstByte (after TLS) for the tile request.
  - Storage matrix: in-Worker time to the last byte of a 16 KiB range at a random 4 KiB-aligned offset.
- **Aggregation (end-to-end):**
  - A probe's value is its median over passes; a continent's value is the median of its probes.
  - Traffic weights: EU 62.9 / NA 20.8 / AS 11.0 / OC 3.0 / SA 1.7 / AF 0.6.
  - Paired differences are per probe and pass, with 95% CIs from a cluster bootstrap over probes (2,000 resamples).
  - Continent is Cloudflare's continent of the client colo, so the Middle East falls under AS (or EU).

### Ledger and drop checks

- **End-to-end:**
  - 2,217 cold and cold2 reads, all on distinct chunks, with 0 stale chunks, 0 X-Miss-Path key mismatches and 0 responses that were not misses.
  - 13 failed cold tests were retried with new tiles.
  - 24 cached responses were not HITs (18 MISS, 6 without a header) and are left out of the cached class.
  - 391 cold or warm misses whose D1 lookup went to the primary from outside Western Europe are flagged and kept. Section 3 shows a variant without them.
  - *[verify]* Rechecked from the raw JSONL and the ledger. Each of the 2,217 cold and cold2 keys appears in the ledger only under its own e2e pass, including lines added later by other stages. Cached: 1,638 HITs, all on the exact cold URL, 4.5–13.6 s after it (p50 5.8 s), 98% at the cold request's client colo, none with X-Miss-Path. Probe drift: 94% of cold requests landed on the slot's colo.
- **Storage matrix:** 0 cold samples dropped by the ledger check and no read errors.
- **Sessions:** 468 chunks read on misses, all first listed by the sessions stage. Six reg reads of eeur chunks at OTP had been listed earlier by the matrix stage, so they were not cold.

### The Tigris live-chunk caveat and the 20261102 control

- **Live chunks:** the data copy read every live chunk (`20261101/`) once from Helsinki via t3.storage.dev, 22:04–22:34Z. The e2e `g` host and the sessions read those live chunks.
- **The 20261102 build:** tiles-geo also holds `20261102/`, a complete build of 138,895 chunks written 2026-11-02 18:28–18:49Z.
  - Nothing serves it. I checked on 2026-11-09: tiles-prod (version 258eab65, deployed 2026-11-08) and tiles-dev (version e03a5b4b, prefix `dev/`) both have `DEPLOYMENT_KEY` 20261101. In the repo, 20261102 appears only in tests.
  - The storage matrix used it as `tigris-cold`.
- **Result:** live and never-read chunks behave the same. At the tier in Europe the cold read was 146 vs 144, and the same holds on every continent and on both sides. The copy did not leave the live chunks visibly warm, so the e2e `g` cold numbers are not flattered by it.

## 4. Storage matrix by tier colo

This is the storage read alone, measured inside the probe Worker:
- **tier** = the cached entrypoint at the upper-tier colo where a full miss runs. **client** = the gateway at the client colo where the race-local miss runs.
- Every storage is primed first, so cold and warm exclude connection setup. The first-contact cost is in 4.3.
- **cold** = the first read of a never-read chunk. **warm** = the same range again immediately, in the same isolate.
- Values are median ms, cold/warm; n = calls.

*[verify]* "reg (measured, LOO)" is a tier-only choice, not the map deployed on `reg`. The deployed map pools tier and client samples, and at SIN it picks apac (458) where the tier-only choice is oc (414). ### 4.3 First contact per isolate (prime)

The prime is a request for a key that does not exist, sent before the cold reads.
- **R2:** head() through the binding, with no connection to open.
- **Tigris:** a signed HEAD over a new HTTPS connection, so it includes DNS, TCP and TLS.

What this suggests:
- wnam, enam, weur and oc sit where their hints say.
- eeur behaves like a bucket near Frankfurt: warm 25 ms at FRA against 73 ms at WAW.
- apac behaves like a bucket in Japan: warm 36 ms at NRT and 46 at KIX, against 98 at SIN. Its cold reads are at least 263 ms median everywhere.
- *[verify, refined]* That holds only for warm reads. eeur's cold reads are slower than weur's at FRA itself (156 vs 114 ms, n = 308) and at WAW (176 vs 157, n = 30), so eeur's metadata and cache answer from FRA while its storage sits further away. The cold penalty (median cold minus median warm at each bucket's lowest-warm colos, both sides pooled, n ≥ 5) varies by bucket: weur 48–61 (LHR, AMS), oc 77–98 (SYD, MEL, AKL), wnam 101–116 (LAX, SJC), enam 102–114 (EWR, IAD, ATL), eeur 131 (FRA) and apac 217–286 (KIX, NRT). For comparison, Tigris's live build costs 80 (LHR) to 132 (FRA). The apac penalty is about twice the others. The cause cannot be told from latency (storage vendor, distance or media).

Cold vs warm vs prime by distance from the bucket ("near" = colos whose median warm read is within 1.5x of the bucket's lowest):

- R2 warm reads cost about the prime plus 10–20 ms.
- Far from the bucket, R2 warm reads stay at round-trip time: none of about 15,700 came in under 30 ms, so there is no sign of an edge copy within seconds.
- Tigris serves a just-read range from near the reader.

### 4.5 The measured map on `reg`

- **Version:** 7300a581, deployed 00:07:30Z with `deploy.sh reg grid/reg-map-final.json`.
- **Coverage:** 308 colos in total.
  - 97 decided on their own samples.
  - 30 pooled with nearby measured colos (within 800 km, widened up to 3,000 km until each bucket has n ≥ 3).
  - 181 unseen colos take the bucket of the nearest seen colo.
- **Rule:** lowest median cold read wins; buckets within max(10 ms, 10%) count as tied, and then the lowest warm read wins.
- **Size:** 200 entries differ from the weur default.
- **Confirmation:** 7 pinned probes all got the expected bucket.

| probe (pinned colo) | X-Miss-Path (colo, via, bucket, pick, storage ms) | race | expected bucket | ok |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Singapore (SIN) | `colo=SIN;via=r2;bucket=apac;pick=map;ms=462` | local | apac | yes |
| Sydney (SYD) | `colo=SYD;via=r2;bucket=oc;pick=map;ms=133` | tier | oc | yes |

### 5.3 Storage matrix per probe city and per client colo

- **Appendix A:** one row per matrix call (1,425 calls from 1,068 cities), with the tier-side cold/warm read of all 8 storages. The failed cities are listed at the end.
- **Appendix B:** the matrix per client colo.
- **CSV:** both sides of every call are in `csv/matrix_per_city.csv`.

## 6. The miss breakdown

**Columns:**
- **pre** = firstByte minus the gateway's handle time: the client round trip and, for R2, the wait for the first body byte after get() resolves.
- **tier hop** = the Tiles fetch minus D1 minus storage.
- **local wait** = the 120 ms race.
- **storage** = the Tigris range GET until response headers, or R2 get() until it resolves.

### 7.3 Sessions (Europe)

- The EEUR replica in FRA served FRA, CDG, WAW and OTP. The WEUR replica in LHR served AMS, LHR, HEL and part of ARN.
- The primary in MAD answered 13–18% of lookups: always from MAD and MRS, and 41–44% from ARN (55 ms on the primary vs 34 ms on the LHR replica).
- No sessions lookup met the "primary from outside WEUR" flag.

### 7.4 Separate D1 placement trial (d1place, 23:49Z–00:53Z, context only)

Workers pinned near each city with a `[placement] region` (an AWS or Azure region) sent `SELECT 1` bursts. Where no replica answered, the rate stepped from 10 to 30 to 50 queries per second, 15 minutes per step (`d1place/logs/driver.log`):

| location | queries | answered by | p50 ms |
|---|---|---|---|
| SIN | 37,400 | APAC replicas in NRT and HKG from the first query | 47–73 |
| NRT | 37,400 | APAC replica in NRT from the first query; from 00:36Z mostly the WEUR primary | 7–8, then 232 |
| SYD | 89,600 | WEUR primary in MAD throughout, at 10, 30 and 50 qps | 295–297 |
| MEL | 89,600 | WEUR primary in MAD throughout, at 10, 30 and 50 qps | 282–284 |
| JNB | 108,000 | WEUR primary in MAD throughout, at 10, 30 and 50 qps | 148–149 |

So in Oceania and South Africa, extra load did not bring up a D1 replica during this run.

## 8. Sessions

- **Setup:** 4 batches of 40 sessions from each of two vantages, all on the 20 sim3 cities (8 EU, 5 NA, 4 AS, 2 OC, 1 SA).
- **Pairing:** each session played on prod, g, one and reg at the same time, so every comparison is paired.
- **Vantages:** this machine in London (client colo LHR) and the Helsinki box, which lands on the WAW client colo. Both are in Europe, so this section says nothing about other continents.
- **Batches 2–4 are not fresh:** they re-request 20–51% of tiles already requested at the same vantage, and their HIT share rose from 5% to 53%. Only batch 1 is close to fresh tiles. Paired results stay fair because all four hosts got identical requests.

View completion (ms; views needing at least one new tile; n = 1,849 per host, first views n = 314):

| host | views | p50 | p90 | p99 | mean | first view p50/p90/mean (n) | views with a non-200 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

#### Per-update and per-bucket costs

- **One data update:**
  - R2, 1 bucket: 138,883 PUTs, $0.62.
  - R2, 6 buckets: 833k PUTs, $3.75.
  - Tigris: $0.69.
  - D1 directory writes: 3.57M rows, $3.57 at the margin (in practice inside D1's included 50M rows).
  - Refill reads at 35.8M: 1.02M GETs, $0.37 on R2 or $0.51 on Tigris.
- **Each extra R2 bucket:** about $4.28/month with live and previous builds in it, or $2.08/month with live only, plus $0.62 per update.
  - Totals at 35.8M by bucket count k: k=1 38.2, k=2 42.5, k=3 46.8, k=4 51.0, k=5 55.3, k=6 59.6.

#### Sensitivities at 35.8M, $/month total (R2 1 bucket / R2 6 buckets / #489 on Tigris)

- **No race (r=0):** 34.8 / 56.2 / 36.7.
- **Worst measured race (r=0.89):** 39.8 / 61.2 / 43.0.
- **R2 miss-path CPU of 4 ms instead of 8 (R2 rows only):** 37.2 / 58.6 / –.
- **Upper-tier hop billed as a third Worker request (not verified):** 41.4 / 62.8 / 44.1.
- **R2 operations under real account usage,** with Cloudflare's round-up to the next million:
  - Class B: $0 at 13.7M and $1.80–2.52 at 35.8M.
  - Class A: $0 unless a month has more than ~970k PUTs (a 6-bucket update plus a second one).
- **Previous build kept in Infrequent Access:** saves about $0.50 per bucket per month net.

**Cost-stage bottom line:**
- One R2 bucket is the cheapest way to run #489: about $1.7/month less than #489 on Tigris at 13.7M requests, and $2.7 less at 35.8M.
- It still costs $8.8/month more than today at 35.8M. Almost all of that extra comes from #489 doubling the billed Worker requests, not from storage.
- Six buckets add +$21.4/month over one bucket, or +$11.0 if the previous build is kept in only one bucket.

**This PoC's spend:**
- The data stage spent about $4.30, including the first day of storage.
- The cost stage puts the total at:
  - $1.0–5.8 if the buckets are deleted on 11-09;
  - $1.5–6.3 if deleted on 11-10;
  - $12.7–17.5 if they are left for 30 days.
  
  The range is whether the account's free 1M Class A covered the 833k PUTs.
- The matrix, e2e and sessions stages each reported under about $0.05.
- tiles-poc-r2-d1warm costs about $0.056/h while it runs.
- Everything stays under the $20 budget if the buckets go within days.
- *[verify, corrected]* The totals above leave out tiles-poc-r2-d1warm. It has run since 22:51Z (still running at 03:09Z), at about $0.056/h, i.e. $1.35/day or about $40 for 30 days. That alone would break the $20 budget, so it must go with the buckets. Later D1 trials by other agents (ledger stage `d1r-dom-*`) are not costed here.

## 10. Caveats, and what was not measured

1. **Tigris connection reuse in production was not measured, and it decides the result.** The PoC hosts had no real traffic, so g's cold misses usually opened a new HTTPS connection to Tigris.
   - That cost about 109 ms over a primed read; R2 binding reads have no such cost.
   - On cold2, Tigris's read was 81 ms faster [−97, −54] and the weighted lead shrank to −38 [−69, +7].
   - The warm-connection model (not a measurement) gives g 293, reg 321, one 398.
   - So R2's measured −115 ms is an upper bound for production.
   - *[verify]* A few misses per second at one tier colo did not warm Tigris connections in the PoC. At FRA, g tier cold reads after another g tier miss there less than 3 s earlier took 238 ms median (n = 62), against 132–140 ms primed. Production FRA would see about 0.5 full misses per second (cost-model volumes), similar to the PoC's rate during passes. Reuse showed up only for the same probe's follow-up (cold2), so in production it likely depends on users' bursts of tiles. The warm-connection model is the best case for Tigris; cold (−115) and cold2 (−38 [−69, +7]) bracket the likely gap.
2. **Thin samples outside Europe and North America:** Oceania 11 probes, South America 9, Africa 5. Many CIs there cross zero.
   - Per-colo rows with n = 3 (one probe × 3 passes) are only indicative. The 30 busiest colos have 9 samples per host.
   - In the matrix, one call per city is noisy: for the 357 cities measured twice, the mapped bucket's cold read differed by a median of 92 ms (p90 331).
3. **China is not covered.** workers.dev is blocked there: 26 of the 41 failed matrix cities are Chinese.
4. **D1:** no Oceania replica ever served, despite the warmer (BNE Durable Object at K=20, briefly 50).
   - Every Oceania lookup went to the primary in Madrid, about 292 ms, on every host.
   - South America went to the primary 24–33% of the time (GIG always).
5. **The R2 "cold" class:** R2 cold reads are of objects the copy wrote from WAW 1–4 h earlier and that nothing had read since. Write-side caching cannot be ruled out, but none is visible (no far warm read under 30 ms).
6. **Tigris "cold" in e2e and sessions means "not read by the PoC since the copy".** The never-read 20261102 control in the matrix behaved the same as the live chunks. The sessions' g FRA median of 9 ms still suggests Tigris holds European copies of chunks once they have been read.
   - *[verify]* Production traffic also reads the live chunks and is not in the ledger. The e2e tiles sit near colo cities, so g's "cold" chunks may have been read by real users shortly before, while nothing else reads the R2 buckets. If that warmed Tigris, it favoured g. No sign of it shows: g's e2e cold reads were slower than the matrix's primed reads, not faster. The matrix comparison is paired within each call and shows no difference (median live − never-read: EU +0, NA +8, AS −8, OC +18 ms).
7. **Sessions are Europe only, and batches 2–4 re-use tiles.** They say nothing about APAC, Oceania or the Americas.
8. **R2's storage time understates its read slightly.** It is measured until get() resolves; the first body byte lands in "pre" (R2 pre 54–65 vs g 42). firstByte is end to end, so the totals are unaffected.
9. **Placement can only be inferred from latency.** eeur reads fastest from Frankfurt and apac from Japan. Whether R2 storage runs on Oracle or on Cloudflare's own hardware is not stated in the docs and cannot be told from these timings.
10. **Not measured:**
    - production traffic patterns, and how often the race fires on R2 in production;
    - whether the upper-tier hop is billed as a third Worker request;
    - a 3-bucket layout (the cost stage prices it; no host ran it);
    - the R2 Infrequent Access class;
    - anything after 02:43Z, so the APAC evening peak and the European day are not covered;
    - *[verify]* a custom domain: every host ran on workers.dev, and the Workers Cache upper tiers seen there might differ on the tiles.immich.cloud zone;
    - prod in the same window, so R2 against today's prod is only a cross-run estimate (section 11).
11. **The comparison with the earlier prod figure (371 ms) is not like for like:** that used 48 probes, a different tile choice and a different time window. *[verify, corrected]* The aggregation is the same (per-probe median, continent median, the same weights; the e2e script follows flypoc's analyze.py), not a different one. See section 11 for a colo-matched comparison.
12. **Still running or deployed:**
    - tiles-poc-r2-d1warm and its logger;
    - the six buckets (833 GB, about $0.42 per day);
    - the uploader, g, one, reg, probe and d1place Workers.

    All are recorded with delete commands in `r2poc/resources.jsonl`. Nothing was written to tiles-geo, D1, tiles-prod, tiles-dev, Terraform or GitHub.

## 11. Comparison with the Fly PoC of 2026-11-08 *[verify]*

Different window (Sunday 13:51–16:53Z vs Monday 00:21–02:10Z), probe set (48 vs 185) and tile picker. The aggregation is the same. Restricted to the 33 client colos both runs measured, never-requested (cold) firstByte, traffic-weighted (`verify/fly_compare.py`):

| run | host | weighted | EU | NA | AS | OC | SA | AF |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Fly PoC (48 probes) | prod | 366 | 249 | 476 | 654 | 808 | 737 | 299 |
| Fly PoC | g (#489 on Tigris) | 506 | 440 | 534 | 712 | 916 | 594 | 429 |
| Fly PoC | ft (Fly fleet behind Workers Cache) | 167 | 150 | 175 | 233 | 126 | 312 | 225 |
| Fly PoC | ftr (ft + race) | 165 | 164 | 150 | 193 | 109 | 306 | 206 |
| this run (primary probes on those colos) | g | 435 | 414 | 385 | 550 | 601 | 754 | 574 |
| this run | one | 368 | 233 | 480 | 673 | 1134 | 685 | 354 |
| this run | reg | 319 | 227 | 351 | 660 | 514 | 747 | 392 |

- g, the one host in both runs, was 71 ms faster in this window. Against each run's own g: reg −116, one −67, ft −339, ftr −341.
- **R2 per colo vs Fly:** 152 ms slower raw (319 vs 167), 223 ms when each is taken against its run's g. The Fly hosts also skip the D1 lookup, which removes Oceania's ~290 ms primary penalty.
- **R2 vs today's prod:** not measured here. Across runs reg (319) sits about 50 ms below prod's 11-08 value (366), which is within the 71 ms window shift seen for g, so the PoC cannot say whether R2 beats prod on misses.
- **Cost at 35.8M:** R2 $38.2 (1 bucket) to $59.6 (6 buckets), or $46.8–51.0 for the 3–4 bucket layouts that give nearly all of `reg`'s benefit, against Fly ft $118 (3 regions) to $180 (5 regions).

## 12. Verification log *[verify]*

Recomputed independently from `e2e/raw/e2e.jsonl`, `grid/raw/grid_{local,box}.jsonl`, `shared/used_chunks.tsv`, the cost scripts and the saved price pages (scripts in `r2poc/verify/`).

**Reproduced:**
- Traffic-weighted and per-continent cold/warm/cached/cold2 levels and paired differences (3.1, 3.2): identical, CIs within 1–2 ms (bootstrap seed). The exception is SA, where the bootstrap moves tens of ms.
- Miss breakdown (6.1, 6.2): identical. Warm-connection model (6.3): g 293 / one 398 / reg 321.
- Storage matrix by continent and per tier colo (4.1, 4.2), Tigris live vs never-read, and the location-hint signatures (4.4).
- Ledger: 0 stale cold chunks in the e2e (2,217) and in the matrix (22,800 reads, no (storage, chunk) read twice), and no other-stage line on any e2e cold key.
- D1 warmer table (section 2) and the D1 routing by continent (7.1).
- Cost tables: `final.py`, `tables.py` and `extra.py` rerun to identical output. Prices match the saved pages: R2 $0.015/GB-month, $4.50/M Class A, $0.36/M Class B; Workers $0.30/M requests and $0.02/M CPU-ms, with footnote 4 billing Workers Cache hits; D1 $0.75/GB-month; Tigris $0.02 per binary GB-month (the page defines 1 GB = 2^30 B), $0.005/1k Class A, $0.0005/1k Class B.

**Corrected:**
1. 3.1, the "excl. D1 primary far" rows: Oceania was scored 0 ms with weight 3.0. Renormalised: cold 418/366/317 (was 405/355/307), warm 252/217/179 (was 245/210/174), cold2 344/359/293 (was 333/349/284), and paired differences and CIs ×1.031 (e.g. cold reg−g −110 [−136, −88], was −107 [−132, −85]). The same bug is in `e2e/scripts/analyze_e2e.py` (weighted sums use `med(vals) or 0`).
2. Section 1, India: "every bucket in 570–770 ms" → 615–734 ms at BOM for Indian probes (622–766 over all BOM-tier calls); "South America and India 700–900 ms" → SA 734–778, Indian colos 712–1,067.
3. Section 10 item 11: the earlier prod comparison used the same aggregation, not a different one.
4. Section 9, PoC spend: the totals left out tiles-poc-r2-d1warm (about $1.35/day, about $40 for 30 days).
5. Section 2, warm class: the D1 lookup never came from memory, and only 44–49% of warm requests ran at the cold request's exec colo.

**Refined or added:**
6. Section 1 and 3.2: Asia's "no difference" is a probe-mix artefact. In East/Southeast Asia, Tigris is faster on cold (+44 [+7, +117]) and cold2 (+163 [+32, +297]).
7. Section 4.2: the LOO column is a tier-only choice. With the deployed map, AS is 422/95 and the weighted tier read 174.
8. Section 4.4: eeur is FRA-like only for warm reads. Per-bucket cold penalties added; apac's is about twice the others.
9. Section 1 and 10: Tigris connection reuse did not appear at FRA with misses less than 3 s apart, so the warm-connection model is the best case for Tigris.
10. Section 2: the D1 warmer did not demonstrably change any routing.
11. Section 3.3: per-continent reg−g swings between passes.
12. Section 9: bucket-layout table; four buckets reach `reg`'s read latency.
13. Section 10: the production-traffic warming of Tigris live chunks (not in the ledger; it would favour g) and workers.dev vs a custom domain added as caveats.
14. Section 11: comparison with the Fly PoC.

**Checked and found sound:** cached responses are real HITs on the exact cold URL. Warm requests are full misses (X-Miss-Path with the same key on all 1,662). Cold chunks are fresh per the ledger, with no reuse across hosts, stages or sessions. R2 shows no edge copy (0 far warm reads under 30 ms). All hosts were unchanged during the runs (report stage, wrangler).

**Could not verify:** R2's storage vendor (Oracle or Cloudflare), physical bucket placement, production Tigris connection reuse, and whether production traffic had read the e2e's Tigris chunks.

## Files

Paths below are relative to the research data directory, which is kept outside the repo (raw Globalping JSONL, CSVs and scripts; available on request). The throwaway Workers and buckets were deleted after the run.

- Report: `r2poc/report/r2-vs-tigris-report.md`, built by `r2poc/report/scripts/build_report.py` from `scripts/template.md`. It formats stage outputs and recomputes nothing.

## Appendix A. Storage matrix, one row per call (per probe city)

- **Read at the tier colo:** each cell is cold/warm ms of one 16 KiB range read (one sample each), after priming.
- **Columns:**
  - "group" is the Globalping region (ME = Western Asia).
  - "client" and "tier" are the colos the call reached.
  - "best R2" is the fastest of the six buckets in that call.
  - "D1" is the replica that answered the tier's `SELECT 1`, with its ms.
- **Ordering:** by group, country, city, then pass.
- **Client-side reads:** in `csv/matrix_per_city.csv`.
